Native OutputRoot publication is atomic per file, not across the artifact set (src/publication.rs:168-225; jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/__main__.py:126-133; jubarte-wasm/cli/bin/jubarte-redlines.mjs:106-110).
- Assigned discovery-0026 architecture (supplied Standard context): Native Markdown images use --resource-path or input parent (fallback .), decode escapes, reject URL schemes and require canonical containment plus capability-relative read. Python/Node CLIs reject resource_path (src/bin/jubarte.rs:2811-2878,2937-2943; jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/__main__.py:1057-1064; jubarte-wasm/cli/bin/jubarte-redlines.mjs:724-727).
- Assigned discovery-0026 architecture (supplied Standard context): Native rendering accesses ambient fonts independently of MCP root: macOS $HOME/Library/Application Support/jubarte/fonts; Linux ${XDG_DATA_HOME:-$HOME/.local/share}/jubarte/fonts; Windows %APPDATA%/jubarte/fonts. JUBARTE_FONT_DIR overrides these. Index defaults to parent(font-directory)/font-index.tsv; JUBARTE_FONT_INDEX overrides or empty/off disables it. Cached face paths are read after stamp checks; saves write a sibling filename ending .tsv.<pid>.tmp then rename (src/convert/font.rs:2637-2652,3098-3107,3154-3229).
- Assigned discovery-0026 architecture (supplied Standard context): Explicit native self-update transfers release-publisher authority into executable replacement. Repository/platform/tag, SHA256SUMS.txt and regular-file extraction are configured; RELEASE_KEYS=[] and FIRST_SIGNED_RELEASE=None leave signature enforcement disabled (src/update.rs:25-48,87-108,186-258,299-321).
- Assigned discovery-0026 architecture (supplied Standard context): Tag-triggered GitHub release consumes build artifacts, optionally signs using step-scoped ZIPSIGN_PRIVATE_KEY staged under $RUNNER_TEMP/zipsign.<random>/keyN with exit cleanup, and publishes dist/* using contents:write. Local release additionally publishes cargo/npm/PyPI packages using operator credentials (.github/workflows/release.yml:15-21,230-321,356-369; scripts/release.sh:374-380,919,935-954,1069).
- Assigned discovery-0026 architecture (supplied Standard context): Pinned external Jules review and companion-fix actions have distinct authority. Review gets PR/check writes with enable_approve=false. Companion invocation rejects fork heads, requires configured trusted author/dispatcher arthrod and qualifying events, then sets require_plan_approval=false and AUTO_CREATE_PR. External action internals are absent. Self-hosted CI/docs execute repository code under runner authority (.github/workflows/jules-review.yml:17-50; .github/workflows/jules-el-conejo-review.yml:69-92,259-324; .github/workflows/ci.yml:13-48; .github/workflows/docs.yml:76-99).
- Assigned discovery-0026 architecture (supplied Standard context): Verified resource-row details retained from the independent architecture review: native cloud fonts derive from $HOME/Library/Group Containers/UBF8T346G9.Office/FontCache/4/CloudFonts/<single-normal-component-family>; renderer and rendered output receive font bytes under OS permissions (src/convert/font.rs:2225-2236). The persistent font index is read by the native renderer/current account and defaults to macOS ~/Library/Application Support/jubarte/font-index.tsv, Linux ${XDG_DATA_HOME:-~/.local/share}/jubarte/font-index.tsv, or Windows %APPDATA%/jubarte/font-index.tsv; JUBARTE_FONT_DIR changes the default parent and JUBARTE_FONT_INDEX separately overrides/disables it (src/convert/font.rs:2637-2652,3098-3107). Python CLI uses selected Path.write_bytes destinations, Node CLI selected fs.writeFileSync destinations; these recipients are local filesystem readers under operator/OS authority, distinct from MCP OutputRoot (jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/__main__.py:126-133; jubarte-wasm/cli/bin/jubarte-redlines.mjs:106-110). Updater's selected archive binary path is jubarte-<version>-<target>/jubarte[.exe], with SHA256SUMS.txt from the fixed repository (src/update.rs:25-48,104-108,198-207). Release private-key values are represented only by the ZIPSIGN_PRIVATE_KEY secret reference; the signing step and zipsign process receive temporary key files, then the GitHub publisher receives dist/* through GH_TOKEN using contents:write (.github/workflows/release.yml:261-309,356-369).
- Document producer -> engine parser. Input bytes pass admission before supported package workflows: compressed-size/entry precheck, shared ZIP structural-defect checks, safe/case-insensitive unique part names, encryption/compression restrictions, counting inflation and XML checks. The expected invariant is bounded, coherent package interpretation without extraction or external-reference fetching. The maintainer debug path deliberately uses unchecked ZIP opening to report otherwise refused packages. Evidence: src/admission.rs:439-575; src/opc/archive.rs:15-51.
- Raw XML caller -> DOM. Checked try_parse_xdocument/validate_xml APIs enforce input policy, while infallible parse_xdocument deliberately does not; library consumers must select checked APIs for untrusted raw XML. Evidence: src/xmllinq/parse.rs:98-129,910-919; SECURITY.md.
- Legacy DOC producer -> distinct compound-file parser. Legacy conversion is supported by read/doc_to_docx/doc_to_markdown and is not a DOCX admission success path. It opens WordDocument and the FIB-selected 0Table/1Table streams and validates their internal structures. DOCX admission independently rejects OLE inputs as LEGACY_DOC. Evidence: src/legacy_doc.rs:172-205,225-235; src/admission.rs:247-264.
- Agent/caller edit plan -> document mutation. The engine checks schema version, optionally verifies source_sha256, requires a nonempty author and refuses signed/macro-bearing packages. The source digest is an optional freshness guard, not authentication or proof of human approval. Native CLI adds filesystem identity checks and fresh source hashing before publication. Evidence: src/edit.rs:2073-2105; src/bin/jubarte.rs:1598-1648,1748-1823.
- MCP client -> document filesystem capability. Startup resolves --root and opens _native._OutputRoot once; path canonicalization is diagnostic, while actual reads/publication use capability-relative native operations. Output names receive portable-path validation; confined publication rejects output symlinks, defaults to no overwrite, stages all files, uses hard-link no-clobber installation or rename on overwrite and reports partial commits. ToolAnnotations are hints, not separate authorization. Every connected stdio client gets registered read/write tools; there is no demonstrated per-client or per-tool identity/tenant policy. Evidence: jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:54-55,67-108,152-218,373-381; jubarte-python/src/lib.rs:1244-1276; src/publication.rs:146-200,228-273,448-505,535-570.
- Native CLI caller -> ambient filesystem publication, distinct from MCP confinement. The caller supplies output paths; held parent directory handles, private Unix staging directories and verified staged bytes protect publication mechanics. --force authorizes replacement but edit routes separately refuse input aliases and recheck original source bytes. Individual commits can succeed before a later artifact fails. Evidence: src/publication.rs:203-225,430-505,535-570; src/bin/jubarte.rs:1607-1648,1748-1823.
- Python/Node CLI caller -> ambient host filesystem. Python _write uses Path.write_bytes; Node uses fs.writeFileSync after an exists/force check. Native staged-publication guarantees must not be assumed for these independent writers. Browser WASM's core API returns bytes, and native publication is compiled out for wasm32. Evidence: jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/__main__.py:126-132; jubarte-wasm/cli/bin/jubarte-redlines.mjs:52-79,106-110; src/lib.rs:163-165; jubarte-wasm/src/lib.rs:7-28.
- Markdown document image reference -> native CLI resource read. The trusted resource root is --resource-path or the Markdown input directory; image names are percent-decoded, URL schemes refused and canonical destinations checked under that root. Reads then use a held OutputRoot capability so a symlink swap cannot redirect outside it. Rust DocxOptions defaults to no caller image loader; custom callbacks carry caller-granted authority. Evidence: src/bin/jubarte.rs:2811-2878,2937-2938; src/markdown/mod.rs:196-198,216-229.
- Native renderer -> ambient font discovery/index. Requested font names influence lookup in configured user/system/Word font folders; Word cloud-family paths require a single normal path component. Cached face paths are checked using stored stamps before reading. These operations use std::fs, not the MCP OutputRoot. The font index persists family/path metadata and writes a temporary sibling file. Evidence: src/convert/font.rs:2225-2235,2241-2315,2637-2652,2855-2862,3090-3107,3154-3169,3182-3229. macOS installed reads include /System/Library/Fonts/Supplemental, /Library/Fonts, /Applications/Microsoft Word.app/Contents/Resources/DFonts, /Library/Fonts/Microsoft, /System/Library/Fonts and $HOME/Library/Group Containers/UBF8T346G9.Office/FontCache/4/CloudFonts/<family>. Windows installed reads include ${WINDIR or SystemRoot or C:\Windows}\Fonts and optional %LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\Windows\Fonts.
- Explicit updater invocation -> release service -> installed executable. Noninteractive install requires --yes or --check before network lookup; --check does not install; explicit --version permits downgrade. GitHub repository and binary target are fixed by code, checksums are configured, signing depends on compiled constants, and extracted output must pass symlink_metadata().is_file(). Dependency-owned HTTPS, extraction and replacement internals were not inspected in this pass. Evidence: src/update.rs:120-124,144-150,186-257,307-320.
- Tag writer/build artifacts -> release-job publication. v* tag pushes trigger version equality checking, platform builds and wheels. A separate GitHub-hosted release job has contents:write, gathers workflow artifacts into dist/, optionally signs them using a step-local secret decoded under $RUNNER_TEMP/zipsign.<random>/keyN, cleans that directory, computes SHA256SUMS.txt and publishes dist/* to the repository release. Evidence: .github/workflows/release.yml:15-49,51-89,230-263,278-321,356-369.
- Operator -> local registry publishing. scripts/release.sh binds a version tag to checked build provenance, requests typed tag confirmation unless YES is enabled, updates main/pushes the tag, then separately publishes crates.io, both npm packages and PyPI. PyPI consumes UV_PUBLISH_TOKEN and dist/pypi/*; npm uses logged-in credentials and optional NPM_OTP; source/tag/artifact checks and registry readbacks are workflow controls, not server-side approval evidence. Evidence: scripts/release.sh:870-901,919,931-956,1044-1069,1095-1097.
- Repository PR metadata/code -> privileged automation. Jules PR Review skips forks/Dependabot, uses a pinned external action, sends API credentials and grants PR/check writes while disabling approval. The companion-remediation workflow separately checks same-repository PRs, runs static analysis and comments, then restricts session creation to TRUSTED_USERS=['arthrod'] under selected events; its pinned external action receives a prompt, starting branch, JULES_API_KEY, require_plan_approval=false and AUTO_CREATE_PR. These are distinct grants; external action/service enforcement is absent from this repository. Evidence: .github/workflows/jules-review.yml:19-50; .github/workflows/jules-el-conejo-review.yml:46-54,65-92,159-180,259-324.
- PR-controlled source -> CI runner. Several pull-request jobs execute on self-hosted runners with repository read token permissions and checkout credential persistence disabled; runner filesystem/network isolation is outside the inspected source. Evidence: .github/workflows/ci.yml:13-36; .github/workflows/docs.yml:42-86.
- Assigned discovery-0024 finding fdf053bb-96cd-4b24-9a57-e4e127bfca82:8 qualifies the Markdown-resource boundary: decoded absolute/prefixed UNC input can replace root during join and trigger a Windows canonicalize remote open before strip_prefix rejection. Held OutputRoot protects the later read but cannot undo that ambient side effect. Conversion, mixed comparison/paragraph patch and text-only image-refusal diagnostics share image_policy.
- Untrusted document bytes -> in-process ZIP/OPC/XML/rendering. Admission precedes representative public parsing paths and applies active resource/XML limits. Inspection uses defaults; PDF/PNG layout uses admit_package and compare limits. Checked XML validates before DOM; infallible parse_xdocument deliberately does not. Sources: src/admission.rs:13-28, src/inspect.rs:604-622, src/convert/mod.rs:768-778, src/document_comparer.rs:4922-4928, src/xmllinq/parse.rs:84-135.
- ZIP central/local records -> shared production opener. open_archive runs zip_defects before returning; unchecked opening is distinct diagnostic/admission machinery. Sources: src/opc/archive.rs:15-20,34-51.
- Stdio MCP client -> filesystem host. --root defaults to cwd, is expanded/canonicalized, native _OutputRoot opens once. Python containment is diagnostic; actual reads/writes use held capability. Outputs additionally validate portable relative names, refuse symlinks, use atomic hard-link no-clobber without overwrite. Tool annotations are hints, not separate caller authorization. Sources: jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:54-55,67-108,373-381, jubarte-python/src/lib.rs:1244-1278, src/publication.rs:146-199,228-254,448-463,558-570.
- Native CLI operator -> ambient input/output paths. std::fs::read inputs and publish_paths holds selected destination parents, stages .jubarte-<uuid>.partial/bytes. Atomic per file, not whole set. Distinct from MCP root confinement. Sources: src/bin/jubarte.rs:2740-2742,261-273, src/publication.rs:168-173,203-225,418-482.
- Python/Node CLI operators -> ambient files. Python Path.read_bytes/write_bytes and Node fs.readFileSync/writeFileSync are independent I/O. Shared grammar/core does not imply native staged publication. Sources: jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/document.py:141-145, jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/__main__.py:73-89,101-103,126-133, jubarte-wasm/cli/bin/jubarte-redlines.mjs:52-79,106-111.
- Markdown images -> native CLI filesystem reads. Root is explicit --resource-path, otherwise Markdown parent/cwd. Names percent-decoded, URL schemes rejected, canonical containment checked, held OutputRoot performs reads. Library loader policy belongs to custom caller. Sources: src/bin/jubarte.rs:2811-2871,2936-2943.
- Native renderer -> ambient font discovery/cache. Document families drive installed-font lookup; cloud family requires one normal component. Index load/save and indexed face reads use ambient std::fs, independently of MCP capability. Sources: src/convert/font.rs:2219-2236,2258-2315,2633-2652,3154-3169,3182-3229,3395-3432.
- Browser/WASM host -> byte APIs. Native publication is non-wasm32 only, WASM engine defaults/self-update disabled. Node CLI adds ambient filesystem operations around WASM. Sources: src/lib.rs:147-149,163-165, jubarte-wasm/Cargo.toml:18-32, jubarte-wasm/src/lib.rs:86-104, jubarte-wasm/cli/bin/jubarte-redlines.mjs:52-73.
- Updater metadata/assets -> executable. Compiled repository/platform, explicit --version may downgrade, --check avoids installation, noninteractive install requires --yes. SHA256SUMS.txt checked and extracted candidate regular-file required. Signature verification inactive: RELEASE_KEYS empty, FIRST_SIGNED_RELEASE None. Sources: src/update.rs:25-48,91-108,120-150,198-257,274-285,307-315.
- Maintainer tag/workflow -> publication/signing. v* triggers version checking/read-only builds; release job alone contents:write. Step-scoped signing reference decoded to temp files, archives signed and optionally checked with committed keys, checksums generated, dist/* published. Other registries via local script. Sources: .github/workflows/release.yml:15-21,40-89,230-322,356-369, scripts/release.sh:919-959,1069.
- Caller-supplied reference DOCX -> Markdown package assembly -> main/reference XML DOM. Python/WASM reference bytes are passed unchanged through shared options; bounded package reading is distinct from XML admission (jubarte-python/src/lib.rs:1206-1218; jubarte-wasm/src/lib.rs:1438-1449,1482-1488; src/markdown/mod.rs:260-282; src/markdown/package.rs:108-126,516-547).

## Attacker Capabilities

- An attacker can author DOCX ZIP entries, relationship targets/types, XML structures and numeric attributes, embedded media/font bytes, legacy DOC character streams, Markdown and edit-plan values that a caller elects to process.
- An MCP client can invoke registered tools over its existing stdio connection and request reads/writes within the granted root; overwrite=true explicitly permits replacement there. No remote listener or tenant model is established by the source (jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:93-112,373-381).
- Document control does not imply control of host environment variables, font directories/index, release assets, signing credentials, operator checkout or trusted MCP root. No new authority is inferred from already possessing those privileges.
- A document supplier can control DOCX/ZIP/XML, embedded media/fonts, legacy DOC bytes and Markdown; it need not control host accounts or filesystem configuration. Denial-of-service and unintended host access boundaries are relevant for library callers even without a demonstrated public deployment.
- An MCP client can invoke exposed document tools and choose relative paths, plans and overwrite within the operator-granted root. It does not thereby possess arbitrary host-path, network, release or registry authority. Directory contents visible to other MCP clients are not isolated per caller by this adapter (jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:67-108,152-160).
- A same-repository PR contributor can control reviewed source/diff and PR metadata and cause applicable CI/review workflows. Fork PR eligibility, GitHub workflow approval settings and self-hosted runner registration determine additional reachability; no assumption of repository-secret or runner-administrator control is needed or granted by this model.
- A release-asset adversary would need authority over published release artifacts, beyond normal document input. Current checksum-only update trust means archive and matching checksum share the release service's authority; documentation acknowledges this limitation. Signing keys, registry credentials and repository-write tokens are separate capabilities (docs/SELF_UPDATE.md:59-80; src/update.rs:44-48).
- External Jules actions receive explicitly configured credentials and repository context, but their internal authorization, remote session permissions and enforcement cannot be established from this repository's workflow declarations alone.
- A document supplier can control input package structures, XML, media, embedded fonts and document content without already controlling the host filesystem, operator environment or release infrastructure: src/admission.rs:439; src/opc/archive.rs:42; src/xmllinq/parse.rs:134.
- An MCP client can supply tool arguments, document paths, edit plans, output directories and explicit overwrite flags within the granted root; it does not thereby receive ambient CLI authority. The server is started on stdio, without a network listener: jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:75; jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:152; jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:373; jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:381.
- An actor able to replace release archives and their adjacent checksum file has meaningful influence over checksum-only updater installations. Such release-write authority is a separate prerequisite, not an ordinary document supplier capability: src/update.rs:44; src/update.rs:48; src/update.rs:245; docs/SELF_UPDATE.md:59.
- The assigned validated findings additionally distinguish a less-trusted local writer of a plan/index staging parent from a document supplier. A symlink overwrite requires a separate writable shared parent, predictable/observable process PID and victim-writable referent; ordinary document control does not grant those prerequisites.
- A document supplier can control document bytes, ZIP/XML structure, relationships, embedded media/fonts, Markdown content and document metadata that callers choose to process. This does not grant control of trusted environment variables, arbitrary operator-selected paths, release credentials or executable installation decisions.
- A connected MCP client can choose tool arguments, source files and output destinations inside the operator-granted root and request overwrite=true. These are existing tool capabilities; new security impact would require exceeding the root grant, unintended disclosure to another actor, bypassing promised replacement restrictions or attacking the host process.
- A library/WASM caller can choose API input bytes and exposed options, including input-limit overrides. A hosted service may expose a narrower subset, but no such service deployment or tenant model is established by this repository.
- A PR contributor can control PR source/text subject to repository contribution permissions and workflow event gates. Same-repository authorship, trusted-user session gates, self-hosted runner admission and external agent authority must be evaluated separately; fork authors are not automatically authorized for secret-bearing Jules workflows.
- Release-asset modification or privileged tag/release control is a distinct prerequisite for updater supply-chain impact. Ordinary document control does not confer it; checksums fetched with release assets do not independently authenticate the publisher (docs/SELF_UPDATE.md:59-80).
- A document supplier controls package names, relationships, XML, revision/comment content, embedded fonts/images and document complexity submitted to a consumer. This does not imply host configuration, MCP root directory, registry accounts or release asset access (src/admission.rs:13-28; SECURITY.md:36-78).
- A connected MCP client can request supported reads/mutations under operator-selected root and overwrite=true. Tools expose that shared root capability, not a separate tenant identity. Escape would add host access not granted (jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:67-108,152-168,258-277,373-381).
- An edit-plan author controls operations and V1 source-hash inclusion. Author strings and revision metadata are document data rather than authenticated identities. Consistency guards are not authorization (src/edit.rs:2081-2105; src/edit/v2_apply.rs:84-110).
- A malicious local peer may possess filesystem access to writable output/cache directories depending on deployment; this is conditional, not assumed. Native held handles, exclusive staging and MCP path validation are relevant to races when such a peer exists (src/publication.rs:146-148,228-270,420-505).
- Control of GitHub release assets, signing material or registry accounts is a conditional supply-chain starting capability, not ordinary document input. Current checksum-only update authenticity depends on release authority and transport/dependency controls; distinct signing-key compromise is not required because trusted keys are empty (src/update.rs:25-48,198-210,245-257; docs/SELF_UPDATE.md:59-82).
- Document suppliers control package/XML/relationship/font/image/Markdown bytes, not operator environment, MCP root grants or release infrastructure (src/admission.rs:15-28; src/bin/jubarte.rs:2851-2876).
- Authorized MCP clients read/publish inside granted root including explicit overwrite; tools do not grant updater or ambient path authority (jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:67-108,153-217,373-381).
- Control of release assets permits changing archive and adjacent checksum; this is a separate supply-chain starting capability not authenticated by checksum-only policy (docs/SELF_UPDATE.md:59-77; src/update.rs:44-48,245-248).
- A document supplier can control DOCX ZIP/XML, embedded media/fonts, Markdown text/image references or supported legacy DOC bytes that a caller elects to process. They do not thereby control trusted process environment, CLI arguments, MCP root selection, host filesystem permissions or release credentials.
- An MCP client can invoke the registered read/write tools and select paths within the operator-granted root, including overwrite=true. All clients sharing this server share that capability; a meaningful boundary failure would add access outside that grant, rather than merely exercise permitted edits. Evidence: jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:67-108, jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:152-168.
- A library caller may supply permissive XML, increased limits or an image callback; those choices are caller authority, not proof that an untrusted document can alter policy. Resource-exhaustion impact on a shared service requires a host deployment and workload access not supplied here. Evidence: src/admission.rs:103-164, src/xmllinq/parse.rs:88-135, src/markdown/mod.rs:196-198.
- A release-asset attacker would need write access to release artifacts or a separate transport/provider compromise. The current checksum-only updater cannot distinguish coordinated archive/checksum replacement using an independent signing identity, as the documentation explicitly explains. Evidence: docs/SELF_UPDATE.md:59-80, src/update.rs:44-48.
- PR authors control reviewed source and PR metadata within their GitHub permissions. Fork authors are excluded from the privileged Jules paths inspected here; same-repository authors and the trusted companion-remediation trigger have different authority. No claim is made that PR text alone can acquire repository write authority. Evidence: .github/workflows/jules-review.yml:19-31, .github/workflows/jules-el-conejo-review.yml:65-82, .github/workflows/jules-el-conejo-review.yml:259-324.
- A document producer can supply DOCX/legacy DOC/XML, embedded font/image bytes, relationship metadata, text, comments and formatting to an invoking host, without necessarily controlling its filesystem, environment, credentials or process limits (SECURITY.md:38-74; src/admission.rs:439-575).
- An MCP client can invoke the registered read and write tools and choose document-relative paths, output paths and overwrite values within the operator-granted root. The root is shared authority, not tenant separation or per-tool approval (jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:67-108,152-160,258-277,373-381).
- A caller controlling library configuration can deliberately raise InputLimits, omit a v1 source hash, supply an image loader or choose a WASM initialization resource. Such authorized configuration control is not an attacker privilege gain by itself (src/admission.rs:103-144; src/edit.rs:103-106; jubarte-wasm/npm/web/jubarte_wasm.js:2575-2594).
- A release-asset attacker would need authority beyond ordinary document input. With the current empty signing key list, replacing both archive and adjacent checksum is outside SHA-256's authenticity protection; this limitation is documented (src/update.rs:44-48; docs/SELF_UPDATE.md:59-80).
- A local actor manipulating native font index/files, publication parents or deployment environment needs corresponding filesystem/environment access. Impact beyond that existing authority requires a separate victim invocation and privilege/readability difference; none is supplied here.
- A document supplier can control package bytes, ZIP names/metadata, XML nesting/attributes, relationships, embedded images/fonts, document font names and text before a user or embedding application processes them. This does not imply control of host environment, executable installation, release credentials or process privileges (SECURITY.md:38-74; src/admission.rs:13-28).
- An authorized MCP caller can name readable documents and request supported edits/renders anywhere inside the operator-granted root, including overwrite when explicitly set. The caller does not thereby receive ambient arbitrary-path authority; native capability-relative I/O owns that boundary (jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:67-108,153-218; src/publication.rs:146-199).
- An embedding Rust/Python/JavaScript application controls the bytes and options it passes. Trust in library callers is separate from trust in document producers; deployments that accept remote uploads or multi-tenant requests must add their own access controls and resource containment (src/lib.rs:7-35; SECURITY.md:71-85).
- A local CLI user intentionally selects ambient input/output paths under their own OS authority. Such selection is ordinary authorized behavior, not a privilege escalation (src/bin/jubarte.rs:2740-2769; src/publication.rs:203-225).
- A remote document supplier is not presumed able to change JUBARTE_FONT_DIR, JUBARTE_FONT_INDEX, the font index, installed fonts, release assets, tags or registry credentials. Any story requiring such control must establish a separate prerequisite (src/convert/font.rs:2637-2652,3100-3107; src/update.rs:25-48).
- A lower-trust contributor can submit PR-controlled code; execution depends on external GitHub run admission. Source PR workflows select self-hosted and execute checkout code, while actual disposable provisioning/cache-server policy is unavailable (.github/workflows/ci.yml:13-17,34-48; .github/actions/mbx/action.yml:8-32).
- A document producer controls container records, XML/relationships, text, revision/comment metadata, embedded media/fonts and legacy DOC bytes. This is a parser/resource boundary without proof of a public service. Input budgets/shared ZIP checks/checked XML are existing controls, not proof of complete DoS resistance. Evidence: SECURITY.md:33-80; src/admission.rs:13-28,63-100,439-470.
- An MCP client/model chooses exposed tool arguments, relative paths, plans, output names and overwrite flags within root. It does not thereby control --root, environment/font-index settings, installed binary, signing keys or release credentials. All calls share root; no tenant identities established. Evidence: jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:67-108,152-160,220-228,373-381.
- Untrusted returned text may influence a consuming model, but interpreting it as instructions/granting tools is an external host decision. MCP hints are metadata, not identity enforcement. Evidence: jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:54-55,132-150; docs/adoption/mcp.md:39-41.
- Replacing release assets plus adjacent checksum differs from source/build/signing-key authority. Present updater does not have independent signature trust root. GitHub publication/transport compromise is an additional prerequisite, not granted by ordinary document input. Evidence: src/update.rs:44-48,198-210,245-257; docs/SELF_UPDATE.md:59-80.
- Write access to shared output/font/cache state could influence a privileged consumer but must be established for a deployment. Publication mitigates races with held directories/staging/atomic installation; renderer cache has separate ambient authority. Evidence: src/publication.rs:431-482,535-570; src/convert/font.rs:3154-3188,3213-3229.
- A document supplier can control DOCX ZIP records, XML, embedded media/fonts, relationships, revision/comment text and document font family names before a user or embedding service processes the document. They do not thereby control host environment variables, installed fonts, MCP startup root or release accounts (SECURITY.md:38-74; src/admission.rs:13-28; src/convert/font.rs:2225-2236).
- An authorized MCP client can invoke all registered tools and select files within the server's granted root, including overwrite=true. Root-wide access is the provided capability; no separate tenants, document ownership identities or per-tool authorization layer is established by this adapter (jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:54-108,152-168,373-381).
- A less-trusted producer can supply edit JSON and anchored replacement content to a trusted caller. A failure could corrupt document edits or bypass snapshot/anchor invariants; source_sha256 is optional, and the model must not assume it always exists (src/edit.rs:106,1735-1761,2081-2089).
- A local actor who can modify shared output directories, font directories or font-index state has a materially different starting capability from a document-only attacker. Assess filesystem races and ambient font-cache integrity only with that prerequisite; do not assume document content controls these locations (src/publication.rs:146-148,431-480; src/convert/font.rs:3154-3189,3213-3229).
- An attacker controlling GitHub release assets could alter an archive and adjacent checksum in the current checksum-only updater trust model; this requires release-asset control and an operator invoking installation, rather than ordinary document processing. Documentation explicitly distinguishes corruption detection from independent authenticity (src/update.rs:44-48,245-256; docs/SELF_UPDATE.md:59-80).
- A contributor can propose repository/workflow changes, but no supplied context establishes tag-push rights, protected-branch bypass, registry credentials, release-signing keys or production deployment access. Privileged release impact needs that additional authorization or a separately demonstrated crossing (.github/workflows/release.yml:15-21,230-235,261-264,356-369).
- Document adversary supplies ZIP entries, XML, relationship targets, images/fonts and content chosen for processing by a victim. No assumption of control over host environment, MCP root, outside filesystem, release accounts or signing keys.
- Connected MCP client invokes all granted tools and selects root-relative documents/outputs, including overwrite=true. Root selection is operator authority; no remotely authenticated or multi-tenant deployment is established (jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:67-108,152-159,373-381).
- Document-only attacker must not gain arbitrary host file access, outbound fetching, executable installation or writes outside authorized destinations. Representative relationship consumers distinguish External targets from package bytes instead of fetching them; this is not exhaustive no-network proof (src/document_comparer.rs:100-108; src/opc/mod.rs:766-783).
- Release-asset adversary has a separate capability from a document supplier. Current checksum-only update depends on release-hosting and TLS integrity; signature separation from release-asset control is inactive (src/update.rs:44-48,245-257; docs/SELF_UPDATE.md:69-87).
- Input suppliers control DOCX/ZIP/XML, embedded fonts/images, legacy DOC, Markdown or edit-plan data supplied to corresponding APIs; no operator environment, filesystem ownership or registry credentials assumed. src/lib.rs:17-30; SECURITY.md:36-40.
- An authorized MCP client chooses registered read/write operations, root-relative paths and overwrite flags, but native directory capability independently limits explicit path authority. jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:67-108,152-159; src/publication.rs:153-199.
- Concurrent filesystem writers matter only when deployment grants access to affected directories; publication anticipates replacement races using held handles and no-clobber hard links. src/publication.rs:146-165,558-569.
- Release-asset control can affect explicit checksum-only updates; requires publication compromise or defeat of download trust chain, not document input. src/update.rs:44-48,245-257; docs/SELF_UPDATE.md:59-80.
- Embedding hosts can choose exposed InputLimitOverrides; caller policy authority itself is not a bypass. src/admission.rs:103-108,137-163; jubarte-wasm/src/lib.rs:80-96.
- A document author can supply malformed DOCX/ZIP/XML, legacy DOC, embedded fonts/images, document metadata, and Markdown image destinations to an application that processes such input. The author does not thereby control environment variables, MCP root, process identity, registry credentials, or release keys.
- An MCP client can invoke exposed read and write tools using paths under the granted root, select output directories and overwrite=true, and receive intentionally exposed document contents. No per-user or per-tenant separation is established inside that root; host process launch and tool approval define caller authority.
- A malicious document can cause parsing/rendering work and family lookups, but filesystem image reads in the native Markdown loader remain separately confined to its resource root. Embedding applications may grant more authority through their own ImageLoader.
- A party controlling a release asset or privileged publication account would affect a different supply-chain boundary from an ordinary document supplier. Current checksum-only updating does not provide an independent compiled signing-key identity.
- An attacker who already controls the invoking account's CLI paths or environment has that account's authority; ordinary authorized local I/O is not itself a privilege escalation.
- A distinct local principal with write access to a shared output/plan directory may substitute directory entries while the victim CLI runs, without already being able to modify victim-owned referents elsewhere. Shared-directory use is conditional, not a default deployment assumption.
- Document suppliers control malformed DOCX/XML/media/fonts/Markdown and image references; host environment, root grants, release credentials and installation remain outside this capability (src/admission.rs:13-28; src/bin/jubarte.rs:2851-2876).
- MCP clients invoke exposed read/edit/render/compare/revision tools and may request overwrite=true under granted root; no independent caller role or human-approval gate is established (jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:67-108,152-159,258-327).
- Library callers may override InputLimits, including raising them; this is already their embedding configuration authority (src/admission.rs:103-165).
- CLI/harness callers choose paths under existing OS authority; no new isolation is inferred (src/publication.rs:203-218; jubarte-rust-inproc/src/main.rs:91-99).
- Release asset control affects updater inputs; checksum-only verification depends on release hosting trust, distinct from document submission or compiled signing keys (docs/SELF_UPDATE.md:59-80; src/update.rs:25-48,245-257).
- A document sender controls DOCX/DOC/XML/media/fonts, relationships, Markdown and edit-plan content supplied to a willing processor; this does not imply environment, CLI flags, host permissions, release assets or credential control.
- An MCP client can invoke registered tools, receive text/reports and request overwrite=true within the granted root; no separate tenant identity/read-only tier is established (jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:54-108,126-160,258-277,373-381).
- A local CLI operator chooses ambient paths and resource roots. A different local writer of shared directory entries can redirect unsafe binding writes to files writable only by the operator; document-only control does not supply that local access (src/publication.rs:203-225; jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/__main__.py:403-414).
- A PR contributor can alter proposed local action/build/test/scripts and gains runner OS/private-network authority if GitHub permits the self-hosted run. Approval and host isolation are deployment prerequisites, not proven absent.
- Release-channel control crosses an independent update-integrity boundary; checksums accompanying an archive are not independent publisher authentication. Host environment/index changes, tag-push authority, registry/signing secrets and external app checkout control require separate evidence (src/update.rs:44-48,245-257; docs/SELF_UPDATE.md:59-79).
- A document supplier can provide malformed ZIP/OPC/XML, embedded images/fonts, relationships, Markdown and supported legacy DOC bytes to a caller that chooses to process them. This establishes parser/resource boundaries without implying an exposed network service (src/admission.rs:439-569; src/legacy_doc.rs:13-50; src/lib.rs:17-30).
- A Markdown supplier controls image target strings, but does not thereby choose --resource-path or acquire access outside the caller-granted image root. Actual reads retain capability confinement after canonicalization (src/bin/jubarte.rs:2815-2870,2937-2943).
- An MCP client can choose tool arguments, root-relative document/output names, plans and overwrite=true within the operator-granted root. The client does not choose the server startup root through tool arguments; transport is stdio, and remote HTTP exposure or multiple tenants would require additional deployment code (jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:67-108,153-168,374-381).
- A concurrent local filesystem writer may race destination checks or mutate filesystem names where its existing OS permissions allow. Such access is a separate prerequisite; shared-user authority does not itself establish privilege escalation. Native publisher uses exclusive staging, directory handles and atomic no-clobber linking, whereas Python/npm CLI I/O must be assessed independently (src/publication.rs:419-505,558-569; jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/__main__.py:403-414; jubarte-wasm/cli/bin/jubarte-redlines.mjs:408-426).
- A release-asset attacker would need GitHub release publication authority or compromise of that infrastructure to influence updater bytes and same-channel checksums. Ordinary document control supplies neither release authority nor local invocation of self-update. Installed signing keys are currently absent (src/update.rs:25-48,198-210,245-257).
- A code contributor is not assumed to possess tag push, signing-key, registry or external app deployment authority. Those conditional workflows become relevant only where actual repository/CI/account permissions admit the contributor's code (.github/workflows/release.yml:15-21,230-239,261-309,356-369; scripts/release.sh:101-106,385-388).
- Document supplier controls ZIP names,relationships,XML,embedded font/image,legacy DOC and extracted text,not trusted env/roots/release configuration or CI credentials.
- MCP stdio client can request documented root reads/mutations and explicit overwrite; no remote HTTP or multi-tenant deployment established. Outside-root authority would be new; authorized root access expected.
- Markdown supplier controls resource names relative to independently selected root,not --resource-path. Scheme/confinement controls prevent beyond-root fetch/read.
- PR author supplies build/test/docs code run by applicable jobs subject to actual GitHub approvals. Approval bypass or actual run permission is not assumed.
- Release-asset-only attacker can replace archive and adjacent checksum in current checksum-only model; no operator/signing-key compromise assumed,explicit updater invocation still required.
- Developer benchmark stdin/stdout client supplies local compare paths to existing process authority; no authenticated production server: jubarte-rust-inproc/src/main.rs:5-20,91-98.
- A document supplier can control document/archive/XML/media/font payloads, Markdown image references and document-selected font families, without controlling the host environment, chosen resource root, installed font index, operator account or release infrastructure. Failure at parsing/layout could add denial of service or unauthorized resource access; those impacts require separate validation.
- An MCP caller can read supported documents under the granted root and request exposed editing/rendering writes there, including overwrite=true. This is intentionally granted authority; there is no established tenant or per-tool identity separation. Reading outside the capability or causing writes outside the document root would require a separate control failure or an independently reachable host subsystem.
- An attacker who can only supply input documents is not assumed able to set JUBARTE_FONT_DIR/JUBARTE_FONT_INDEX, modify installed font/index files, publish release assets or push tags. Control of those resources is a distinct prerequisite.
- A release-asset attacker could affect the updater only when the user invokes update and consumes the affected release. Current checksums provide corruption detection without independent signer authentication, as the documentation explicitly recognizes.
- A document supplier may control document package bytes, XML, embedded fonts/images, Markdown and edit-plan content passed by a host. The supplier does not thereby control environment variables, the MCP root, arbitrary CLI flags, release assets, signing keys or operator credentials (SECURITY.md:38-74; jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:67-87; src/update.rs:25-48).
- An authorized MCP client can request registered document reads and writes anywhere allowed by the granted root and tool parameters, including overwrite=true. This already-authorized root access is not a new privilege; a meaningful failure would cross the root or other independently enforced capability (jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:75-108,152-160).
- A caller embedding the Rust/Python library already has its host process's authority. Parser-input compromise could add unauthorized access or computation, but no repository evidence establishes an internet-facing or multitenant deployment (src/lib.rs:7-11; jubarte-python/src/lib.rs:5-15).
- Release-asset replacement becomes relevant only when an operator requests an update; checksum-only authenticity depends on the trusted release channel because an asset replacer can also replace its adjacent checksum. Signing-key or release-workflow compromise is a distinct privileged prerequisite (docs/SELF_UPDATE.md:59-80; src/update.rs:44-48,245-257).
- A separate local filesystem adversary may control entries in an operator-selected shared output/plan directory without permission to write victim-owned files elsewhere. Direct writes and predictable staging add capability if they follow those entries; private directories prevent this scenario (jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/__main__.py:126-133,407-411; jubarte-wasm/cli/bin/jubarte-redlines.mjs:106-112,420-423).
- A document supplier can control package structure, XML, relationships, embedded media/fonts, document text, revision metadata, Markdown or legacy DOC bytes that a caller chooses to process. Such control does not grant host filesystem paths, trusted environment variables, operator credentials or release authority by itself.
- A connected MCP client can request reads and supported document transformations within the server root and select output paths and overwrite flags there. The server intentionally grants that directory authority to its client; there is no evidenced per-tool principal separation or independent read-only client role (jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:67-108,152-159,258-277,373-381).
- An ordinary CLI user selects ambient input/output paths under their process permissions. Merely exercising those already-granted paths is not a privilege escalation; a meaningful failure would require document data or a lower-authority caller to gain additional unauthorized authority.
- A hostile document can consume parser, comparison and rendering resources within effective input limits, including the materially larger comparison/render budgets. Shared-service impact requires a host deployment that accepts attacker inputs and shares finite process resources; no such network service or tenant deployment was supplied (src/admission.rs:63-99; SECURITY.md:71-74).
- An attacker able to replace GitHub release archives and the adjacent checksum file could influence checksum-only update content; that requires release-asset control absent from a mere document supplier. Compiled release signature verification is not currently active (src/update.rs:44-48,245-285; docs/SELF_UPDATE.md:59-80).
- Control of trusted release tags, repository code or maintainer registry credentials is a separate supply-chain actor capability, not something inferred from untrusted document content.
- Input suppliers control DOCX ZIP/XML, embedded fonts/images, legacy DOC, Markdown and caller-accepted edit plans, without automatically controlling trusted environment, arbitrary local paths, credentials or releases (SECURITY.md:38-74; src/admission.rs:439-569; src/legacy_doc.rs:13-50).
- MCP clients select in-root documents/operations, receive derived content and request overwrite=true in-root writes; those intended grants are not additional compromise (jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:67-108,131-217,258-277).
- CLI/library callers already control invocation and explicitly supplied paths under their OS account; escalation requires authority beyond these grants (src/publication.rs:203-225; src/bin/jubarte.rs:2851-2866). A separate local principal may control entries in a shared output/plan directory.
- Release/registry/signing compromise is separate authority; checksum-only updates do not authenticate against a compiled key, but document control does not imply release control (src/update.rs:44-48,245-257; docs/SELF_UPDATE.md:59-80).
- Public-service/tenant effects require deployment separation not established by inspected source; no public HTTP service was established (jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:373-381; SECURITY.md:84-85).
- PR authors control checked-out build scripts/tests/local actions for permitted CI jobs; GitHub workflow approval policy and actual runner lifecycle are external prerequisites (.github/workflows/ci.yml:13-48).
- A document supplier can control document bytes, XML structure and relationships, embedded media/fonts, names and document text consumed by a legitimate caller. They do not thereby control the caller's environment, executable, resource-root selection, font-index file, MCP root or release infrastructure. Relevant boundary failures could add process availability impact, unauthorized local access or output corruption; these are scenarios, not established findings. Sources: SECURITY.md:36-74; src/admission.rs:439-495; src/bin/jubarte.rs:2851-2877.
- An authorized MCP client can request the exposed read and write tools against files under the operator-granted root and opt into overwrite. There is no demonstrated tenant separation within that root and no reason to infer access to arbitrary host paths merely from tool visibility. Sources: jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:38-55,67-108,373-381.
- A caller directly embedding Rust, Python or WASM APIs can choose input limits and callbacks within its own authority. Raising limits or providing an expansive image loader is a caller capability and cannot independently establish privilege escalation. Sources: src/admission.rs:103-165; jubarte-python/src/lib.rs:65-79; jubarte-wasm/src/lib.rs:77-97; src/markdown/mod.rs:159-170.
- A PR contributor can propose repository source that normal CI may build. A fork contributor is excluded from the two reviewed Jules secret-bearing workflows, while same-repository authors and trusted dispatchers have different workflow eligibility. Actual self-hosted runner exposure depends on GitHub settings and runner isolation not present in source. Sources: .github/workflows/ci.yml:13-48; .github/workflows/jules-review.yml:18-26; .github/workflows/jules-el-conejo-review.yml:35-54,65-92,260-270.
- An attacker controlling only release assets would need an operator to invoke installation, or an already configured automation to run --yes, before changed assets reach the executable. Current checksum-only policy does not independently authenticate assets against a signing key, as the documentation explicitly acknowledges; control of release assets is not granted to ordinary document suppliers. Sources: docs/SELF_UPDATE.md:38-41,59-80; src/update.rs:120-124,240-257.
- An input producer can supply document/package/XML/media bytes and content such as relationships, font-family names, tracked-change authors, comments and text, without already controlling the host account, environment, trusted output root, release infrastructure or signing keys (SECURITY.md:38-74; src/convert/font.rs:2225-2235). Boundary failure could add host-process availability impact, unintended filesystem access, or document/output corruption; this model does not claim such a failure.
- An MCP client can select paths and output names within the operator-granted root, submit JSON edit plans, invoke rendering and request overwrite=true. Those are intentionally granted tool operations. Access outside that root, unrelated ambient process capabilities, or cross-client ownership controls must not be assumed authorized merely from tool visibility (jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:67-108,153-168,220-277).
- Native and Node CLI users select local input/output paths and operate with their host account's filesystem permissions. Self-only arbitrary path writes requested by that same operator are not privilege escalation; MCP confinement is a separate boundary (src/publication.rs:203-225; jubarte-wasm/cli/bin/jubarte-redlines.mjs:52-78,106-111).
- A contributor may influence PR source and review inputs, but is not assumed able to push trusted release tags, change branch protections, retrieve CI secrets or alter an installed binary's embedded update policy. Same-repository PR and trusted-user requirements are workflow prerequisites; external action implementation is not available here (.github/workflows/jules-review.yml:19-50; .github/workflows/jules-el-conejo-review.yml:35-52).
- A party able to alter only release assets cannot change public keys compiled into an already installed binary, but current source compiles no trusted keys. The documented staged signature design must therefore be distinguished from the effective checksum-only policy (src/update.rs:44-48,245-249; docs/SELF_UPDATE.md:59-87). No release compromise is alleged.
- Assigned discovery-0026 architecture (supplied Standard context): Document suppliers control package names/metadata, XML/relationships, embedded media/fonts, legacy structures and Markdown text; host environment, chosen trusted roots, release credentials and operator accounts are not implied capabilities.
- Assigned discovery-0026 architecture (supplied Standard context): MCP clients can read/process under-root documents and invoke exposed writes with overwrite=true. Outside-root access would add authority; ordinary inside-root replacement is already granted (jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:67-108,153-159).
- Assigned discovery-0026 architecture (supplied Standard context): Local path-race or font-index adversaries require write access to the relevant directories/index. Document-only control does not establish that prerequisite (src/publication.rs:146-148; src/convert/font.rs:3154-3229).
- Assigned discovery-0026 architecture (supplied Standard context): Library callers can select permissive parsing, budgets and callbacks; CLI operators already select ambient paths. These choices become boundaries only when a host exposes them to less-trusted callers.
- Assigned discovery-0026 architecture (supplied Standard context): Release-asset publishers can replace archives and adjacent sums; current checksum-only updater provides no independent signature restriction. PR submitters control review context but lack the configured companion invocation authority unless its gates pass (src/update.rs:44-48,245-249; .github/workflows/jules-el-conejo-review.yml:259-324).
- An untrusted document author can supply archive entries, XML, relationships, embedded images/fonts, legacy DOC bytes, Markdown and document metadata to a consuming application. They do not thereby control host environment, process permissions, release credentials or trusted resource roots. Parser failure could affect host availability; filesystem/network impact requires a reachable consumer crossing its specific boundary. Evidence: src/admission.rs:13-27,439-575; src/legacy_doc.rs:180-205; src/bin/jubarte.rs:2811-2878.
- An MCP client with access to the server's stdio stream can invoke registered read and write tools throughout the configured root and request overwrite=true. That is already granted authority; modification or disclosure within that root is not by itself an authorization bypass. Additional impact requires leaving the granted filesystem capability or compromising another asset. Evidence: jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:67-108,152-218,258-277,300-327,373-381.
- A concurrent filesystem writer may alter output names, symlinks or source bytes only where existing host permissions allow it. Native held-directory/capability operations and no-clobber commit address different races from lexical prechecks; do not assume equivalent controls in independent Python/Node writers. Evidence: src/publication.rs:146-165,430-505,558-570; src/bin/jubarte.rs:1642-1648,1748-1823.
- A party controlling release assets can replace both a current archive and adjacent checksums; current compiled updater has no signing keys. This prerequisite is release-distribution control, not control available to an ordinary document producer. Evidence: src/update.rs:25-48,198-210,245-257; docs/SELF_UPDATE.md:59-82.
- A PR author controls branch contents and metadata. Same-repository admission, event gates and trusted-author checks determine which automation capability they can reach. Actual Jules action implementation, provider permissions and repository runner policies are not present in this source and cannot be inferred from descriptive comments. Evidence: .github/workflows/jules-review.yml:19-50; .github/workflows/jules-el-conejo-review.yml:35-54,65-92,259-324.
- Document supplier controls ZIP records, OPC/XML, embedded fonts/images, text, Markdown, legacy DOC supplied to an authorized interface. No initial environment, operator-root, executable, release-asset or credential control. Sources: SECURITY.md:38-41, src/admission.rs:13-28, src/legacy_doc.rs:13-50.
- MCP client invokes registered tools with path/plan/render/output/overwrite arguments within operator-granted root. No separate tenants or per-tool principals established. Authorized root reads and requested writes are normal capabilities. Sources: jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:67-108,220-277,373-381.
- Concurrent filesystem writer is conditional publication/resource actor; not assumed for ordinary remote document supplier. Held capabilities/no-clobber installation are relevant controls. Sources: src/publication.rs:146-165,558-570, src/bin/jubarte.rs:2858-2871.
- Release-asset attacker could cross install boundary upon operator update; checksum-only mode has no independent signer. Asset-write is an additional prerequisite, not gained by document input. Sources: docs/SELF_UPDATE.md:59-82, src/update.rs:44-48,198-257.
- Trusted startup environment or font-index control is distinct authority from document supply. Cache/font locations and indexed paths can be selected by that actor; escalation requires a less-privileged writer and useful new effect. Sources: src/convert/font.rs:2637-2652,3100-3107,3314-3358.

## Security Objectives

- Reject malformed or excessive document input before unbounded recursion, scalar-driven allocation or algorithmic amplification reaches the host process.
- Keep document relationship resolution within in-memory package data and do not fetch external references (src/convert/mod.rs:21310-21319; src/admission.rs:14-23).
- Enforce held-root filesystem confinement and no-clobber publication; retain source identity for guarded native edits (src/publication.rs:419-574; src/bin/jubarte.rs:1610-1667,1768-1826).
- Install only checksum-verified release binaries and enforce configured signing keys/version rules and regular-file validation; never expose CI secret material in generated analysis.
- Refuse unsafe or resource-policy-violating untrusted packages before unbounded package/DOM work, while preserving explicit caller obligations for permissive low-level APIs and process-level CPU/memory limits (src/admission.rs:13-28,63-99; src/xmllinq/parse.rs:21-36; SECURITY.md:69-75).
- Keep document relationships from becoming unintended network/filesystem authority; preserve separate explicit image-loader and native font-resource policies (src/admission.rs:20-22; src/bin/jubarte.rs:2811-2870; src/convert/font.rs:2633-2652).
- Keep MCP document reads/writes beneath the held directory capability, refuse unsafe portable output names and default to preserving existing outputs; distinguish per-file atomicity from multi-output transaction success (src/publication.rs:146-199,228-273; jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:91-108).
- Bind supplied edit snapshots to actual source bytes, accurately report refusal/partial publication, and keep author labels and SHA content keys distinct from user authentication (src/edit.rs:2081-2089; src/publication.rs:89-98,123-130; SECURITY.md:77-86).
- Only explicitly requested native self-update may replace the executable; enforce configured checksum/signature policy and regular-file extraction verification without presenting current checksum-only operation as signed-release authentication (src/update.rs:120-124,234-257,307-320).
- Keep release signing, GitHub publication, crates.io, npm, PyPI, review-report writing and remote remediation independently authorized; do not treat prompt instructions or tool annotations as an enforcement substitute (.github/workflows/release.yml:234-235,261-285,356-369; .github/workflows/jules-review.yml:28-50; .github/workflows/jules-el-conejo-review.yml:261-272,320-324).
- Reject malformed or over-budget untrusted packages before expensive document processing; retain host-enforced process limits because admission budgets do not establish a universal CPU/memory bound: src/admission.rs:63; src/admission.rs:87; src/admission.rs:439; src/admission.rs:531.
- Confine MCP document reads/publication to the held root, preserve existing outputs unless replacement is explicitly authorized, and do not treat Python prechecks as the actual I/O authorization: src/publication.rs:155; src/publication.rs:162; src/publication.rs:175; jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:75; jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:101.
- Stage all output bytes before publishing and expose accurate partial-commit state if later commits fail. Publication is atomic per file, not across an output set: src/publication.rs:83; src/publication.rs:123.
- Keep update execution explicit and preserve executable integrity through release selection, checksums, configured signature policy and extracted-file type verification: src/update.rs:186; src/update.rs:198; src/update.rs:245; src/update.rs:255; src/update.rs:308.
- Keep release signing material confined to the signing step and distinguish artifact-build read permissions from release-publication write permissions: .github/workflows/release.yml:20; .github/workflows/release.yml:234; .github/workflows/release.yml:263; .github/workflows/release.yml:278.
- A successful redact/docprops scrub operation must enforce its selected confidentiality postcondition across supported XML encodings and actual property relationship targets before outgoing document distribution; package validity alone does not prove sensitive text/metadata removal.
- Reject malformed/unsupported packages and enforce active resource limits before expensive parsing; do not interpret archive member names as arbitrary extraction paths or execute document macros. Checked ZIP admission and XML policy are component controls, while process CPU/memory isolation remains a host obligation (src/admission.rs:13-28,439-470,531-535; SECURITY.md:69-74).
- Preserve MCP document read/output confinement through held directory capabilities despite path changes, and reject unsafe output paths and output symlinks rather than relying solely on Python resolve checks (jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:75-108; src/publication.rs:153-199,228-273,431-463).
- Preserve existing output bytes through staging failures; report partial commits and durability uncertainty accurately. Do not claim atomic publication across multiple files or native publication parity for Python/Node write helpers (src/publication.rs:100-130,168-174,387-405).
- Keep Markdown image reads inside the selected resource root and avoid automatic network fetches from image targets (src/bin/jubarte.rs:2851-2877,2937-2943). Keep this control distinct from native font discovery and output confinement.
- Reject stale edit snapshots when source_sha256 is supplied, and preserve the documented caller obligation when it is omitted (src/edit.rs:103-106,2081-2089). Revision author fields and SHA-1/FNV comparison keys must not become authentication claims (SECURITY.md:76-85).
- Install only the explicitly selected supported-platform release artifact after applicable checksum, signature-policy and regular-file checks, with the documented interaction/downgrade policy (src/update.rs:91-125,144-160,198-257,307-321).
- Keep release signing, GitHub release writes, registry publishing, PR comments/checks and external remediation sessions as independent capabilities with their own event/account gates and external permission prerequisites.
- When docprops scrubbing is requested, remove identifying properties by authoritative OPC metadata target before returning outgoing package bytes; conventional-path-only sanitation misses relocated parts (src/scrub.rs:357-384; src/opc/mod.rs:655-667).
- Refuse packages/XML violating active policy before unbounded downstream parsing; caller owns process memory/CPU limits and permissive raw XML API obligations (src/admission.rs:13-28,63-99; src/xmllinq/parse.rs:25-36,119-135; SECURITY.md:72-78).
- Keep relationships within package semantics and preserve external targets as data rather than retrieval authority. Representative comparison treats External targets as strings and reads internal part bytes (src/document_comparer.rs:94-108). Broader no-network guarantee requires checking all consumers.
- Keep MCP document reads and explicit artifact writes beneath retained root, refusing escape symlinks, unsafe names and unrequested overwrite; distinguish ambient renderer font/cache I/O (jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:67-108; src/publication.rs:146-199,228-270,448-464; src/convert/font.rs:3098-3107,3213-3229).
- Bind verification/receipt digests to chosen primary bytes and report refused plans without publication. V1 snapshot binding is optional when source_sha256 absent (src/edit/prepared.rs:5-13,157-186; src/edit.rs:2081-2089).
- Preserve existing native outputs on staging failure, commit complete bytes per file and report prior commits on later failure; no all-files atomic transaction is promised (src/publication.rs:83-143,168-174,420-505).
- Executable replacement requires explicit update and configured release/checksum/regular-file controls; implemented signature support differs from current checksum-only configuration (src/update.rs:44-48,186-257,299-321).
- Keep document clients separate from maintainer credentials/signing keys; bind CI releases to v* tag and matching package version with scoped permissions/checksums (.github/workflows/release.yml:15-21,31-49,230-264,317-322,356-369).
- Do not fetch external document relationships or Markdown URL resources; confine MCP and resource reads to granted directory capabilities (src/admission.rs:15-22; src/bin/jubarte.rs:2835-2876; src/publication.rs:146-199).
- Refuse malformed/over-budget packages/XML before DOM processing; host memory/CPU process bounds remain necessary (src/admission.rs:39-99; src/xmllinq/parse.rs:21-36; SECURITY.md:70-85).
- Bind source_sha256 to document bytes when supplied and reject stale plans; staged publication is atomic per file only (src/edit.rs:2081-2089; src/publication.rs:83-131,168-174).
- Protect updater integrity with HTTPS/checksums, configured signatures and regular-file check; keep document processing distinct from release publication (src/update.rs:198-258,299-307; .github/workflows/release.yml:230-275).
- Bound untrusted package/XML resource use and reject unsafe or ambiguous package representations before protected processing; preserve explicit caller-configured limits. Raw permissive parsing and maintainer debug inspection must remain distinguishable from checked admission. Evidence: src/admission.rs:39-164, src/admission.rs:439-553, src/opc/archive.rs:15-19, src/xmllinq/parse.rs:88-135.
- Keep MCP document reads and publication within the persistent root capability despite symlink/path replacement, preserve overwrite opt-in and report partial publication accurately. Evidence: src/publication.rs:83-199, src/publication.rs:431-570.
- Bind prepared edits to the intended source and expose refusal outcomes; do not treat a content digest, selected author string or model-visible tool annotation as authenticated authority. Evidence: src/edit.rs:2081-2092, src/edit/v2_apply.rs:84-100, jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:54-55.
- Prevent Markdown image references from gaining arbitrary host-file or network read authority in the native CLI loader; caller-provided library callbacks own their additional authority. Evidence: src/bin/jubarte.rs:2811-2878, src/markdown/mod.rs:196-198.
- Install only the explicitly selected release's intended regular executable, with the configured checksum/signature policy and operator confirmation semantics. Preserve the distinction between implemented signing support and active trusted signing keys. Evidence: src/update.rs:104-124, src/update.rs:198-258, src/update.rs:307-321.
- Keep release signing, registry publication, review comments and companion PR creation as separately authorized capabilities. Bind published versions/artifacts and constrain credentials to their intended consumers. - Use checked XML parsing for untrusted direct XML, retaining the intentionally permissive parser contract and inclusive configurable tag limits (src/xmllinq/parse.rs:49-57,90-135; SECURITY.md:43-65).

- Refuse malformed or resource-excessive untrusted packages before uncontrolled inflation/DOM construction; preserve active configured limits across consumers. Defaults are 64 MiB compressed/per-part, 256 MiB aggregate, 10,000 entries and XML depth 256; comparison defaults are 512 MiB compressed/per-part and 2 GiB aggregate with the same entry/depth caps. - Assigned discovery-0026 architecture (supplied Standard context): Avoid document-driven network fetching, macro execution and external office processes; confine native Markdown image reads to the caller-selected resource root (src/admission.rs:16-22; src/lib.rs:7-11; src/bin/jubarte.rs:2851-2878).
- Assigned discovery-0026 architecture (supplied Standard context): Confine MCP document I/O, preserve source/intent guards, require explicit replacement and report partial publication accurately; application root checks are not a process sandbox.
- Assigned discovery-0026 architecture (supplied Standard context): Protect executable/release integrity and secret recipients; keep review verdicts separate from human approval and maintain distinct external-action privileges.
- Refuse incoherent, unsupported or over-budget DOCX inputs before unbounded package processing; preserve typed refusals across bindings. Maintain independent ZIP structural checks and bounded actual inflation rather than trusting declared sizes. Evidence: src/admission.rs:439-575; src/opc/archive.rs:34-51.
- Keep document relationships and Markdown URLs from automatically granting external fetch authority. Native Markdown image reads must stay inside the explicitly selected resource directory; custom library image loaders remain caller responsibility. - Maintain MCP document I/O confinement using the held OutputRoot, portable output names and atomic no-clobber installation; do not equate tool annotations or canonicalization alone with authorization. - Apply edits against the intended bytes when a source guard is supplied and preserve explicit output authority during native publication. Preserve visibility of partial commits and durability uncertainty rather than claiming multi-file rollback. - Treat font discovery/index permissions and environment as native-host resources separate from document-output confinement, including native MCP rendering. - Keep self-update explicitly requested, checksum-verified, platform-bound and regular-file verified; faithfully describe current checksum-only trust and conditional future signature enforcement. Evidence: src/update.rs:25-48,120-124,198-257,307-320; docs/SELF_UPDATE.md:59-82.

## Assumptions

- Exact userContext is null; no supplied threat model or CODEX_SECURITY_KNOWLEDGE_BASE was present. Only current on-disk source was reviewed; no Git history, network, application execution or credential validation was used.

Whether a particular published archive was optionally signed cannot be inferred from repository source or secret declarations.

User fonts resolve nonempty JUBARTE_FONT_DIR else $HOME/Library/Application Support/jubarte/fonts; other non-Windows use HOME prerequisite then XDG_DATA_HOME or HOME/.local/share; Windows uses APPDATA/jubarte/fonts, WINDIR else SystemRoot else C:\Windows/Fonts, and LOCALAPPDATA/Microsoft/Windows/Fonts (src/convert/font.rs:2225-2236,2281-2315,2637-2652,2855-2862). - Policy names an rdocx-opc dependency, but the current Cargo.toml uses oxml-opc 0.14.2. Retain both the policy's historical dependency guarantee and the actual dependency graph; package-specific controls must be established in current code rather than inferred from that policy wording (SECURITY.md; Cargo.toml:101-117; src/opc/mod.rs:29-51).
- docs/FILESYSTEM_PUBLICATION.md:9-18 broadly describes 'CLI and plan recorder' using the shared publisher. Native CLI/MCP implement it, but Python/npm CLI _write and recorders currently perform direct adapter-owned writes and PID-partial replacement. Shared flags/core logic do not establish equal filesystem guarantees (jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/__main__.py:126-133,403-414; jubarte-wasm/cli/bin/jubarte-redlines.mjs:106-111,408-426).
- docs/FILESYSTEM_PUBLICATION.md:78-81 calls the native image resolver ambient and warns hosted callers to supply explicit input authority. Current native implementation actually confines image reads through OutputRoot to --resource-path or the Markdown parent, after percent decoding and canonical containment. Caller choice of that root remains trusted configuration (src/bin/jubarte.rs:2815-2870,2937-2943).
- src/lib.rs:7-11 describes complete-byte APIs with 'no temp files', and MCP instructions say tools touch files under root. Native rendering nevertheless maintains an ambient font-index file and temporary sibling outside root under its default per-user configuration. These are font metadata/cache operations; no inspected evidence establishes document-text caching or exfiltration (src/convert/font.rs:3095-3106,3183-3229; jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:5-9,67-72).
- docs/SELF_UPDATE.md:22-24 says GH_TOKEN/GITHUB_TOKEN, when set, lift the API request budget. Current src/update.rs:198-210 supplies neither explicit auth_token nor auth_token_from_env. Installed self_update 1.3.1 source defaults auth_token=None/auth_token_from_env=false; therefore environment authentication is not currently enabled. Dependency source was inspected offline under the local Cargo registry, not treated as a repository-relative citation.
- Current updater signature inactivity agrees with docs/SELF_UPDATE.md:59-82; compile-time RELEASE_KEYS=[] and FIRST_SIGNED_RELEASE=None are the effective values, not the conditional signed-release design. Actual production GitHub signing secret presence and release-asset permissions remain unknown (src/update.rs:44-48; .github/workflows/release.yml:261-309).

- No exact deployment context or authoritative knowledge base was supplied. The model describes supported code paths, not observed production exposure, remote service access, tenant isolation or active release settings.
- Repository SECURITY.md is inherited policy and analysis data. It declares untrusted document inputs and process memory/CPU isolation obligations; this pass does not certify that every exported operation or decoder enforces every declared limit. Evidence: SECURITY.md:39-77.
- Documentation/configuration disagreement: MCP build_server says tools only touch files under root, and its module documentation describes outputs under root, but native rendering has ambient font reads and persistent font-index writes outside that root. Preserve both facts: OutputRoot confines document I/O; the process is not globally filesystem confined. Native macOS fonts default to $HOME/Library/Application Support/jubarte/fonts and index to $HOME/Library/Application Support/jubarte/font-index.tsv. Linux uses $XDG_DATA_HOME/jubarte/fonts or $HOME/.local/share/jubarte/fonts with the index in the parent; Windows uses $APPDATA/jubarte/fonts with index in its parent and system fonts from WINDIR/SystemRoot/LOCALAPPDATA. Nonempty JUBARTE_FONT_DIR overrides fonts; JUBARTE_FONT_INDEX explicitly selects index, empty/off disables it. Temporary sibling replaces extension with tsv.<pid>.tmp. Evidence: jubarte-python/python/jubarte_redlines/mcp_server.py:5-9,67-72,234-236; src/convert/font.rs:2633-2652,2831-2862,3098-3107,3155-3168,3182-3229. No exploit or unauthorized new capability is established by this architectural discrepancy alone.
- Documentation/configuration disagreement: crate overview says every function returns bytes with no temp files, whereas native rendering maintains a font index using a temporary sibling file and rename. This does not imply document bytes are written to that index; it records lookup/path/stamp metadata. Evidence: src/lib.rs:7-11; src/convert/font.rs:3090-3096,3192-3229.
